\begin{document}
\title{Sarvabuddhasamāyogaḍākinījālaśaṃvara\\
(preliminary edition from IEI Ms. SL. 48)}
\author{Arlo Griffiths \\ Alexis Sanderson \\ Péter-Dániel Szántó}
\date{(latest version) \today}
\maketitle
\noindent\textbf{List of abbreviations employed in the register for testimonia}
\begin{description}
\item[\Td] Tibetan translations as transmitted in the sDe dge print.
\item[\anontreatise] An anonymous tantric treatise most likely from the early half of the 9th century surviving only in fragments in Ms. National Archives Kathmandu 3-737 = Nepal German Manuscript Preservation Project reel no. A 37/4. Palm-leaf, ff. xx (the present fragment), Old Newar, undated.
\item[\adhyardhasatika] \textit{Adhyardhaśatikā Prajñāpāramitā}. Ed. by Toru Tomabechi, \textit{A\-dhyardhaśatikā Prajñāpāramitā. Sanskrit and Tibetan Texts}. Sanskrit Texts from the Tibetan Autonomous Region No. 5. China Tibetology Publishing House -- Austrian Academy of Sciences Press, Beijing -- Vienna 2009.
\item[\atmasadhanavatara] \textit{Ātmasādhanāvatāra} of Jñānapāda (fl. late 8th to early 9th century). Partially transmitted in Samantabhadra's \saramanjarip, q.v. %A fragment (corresponding to \Td\ 53b6-57a4, nearly half of the work) surviving in the longer recension of the \textit{Sāramañjarī} by Samantabhadra (fl. 9th century). Ms. listed in \textsc{Sferra} 2008: 45, item 30. Palm-leaf, ff. 2 (the present fragment), Māgadhī, dated to the 5th regnal year of Nayapāla (mid-11th century). % revise
\item[\anavila] \textit{Anāvilatantra} Ms. Tokyo University Library No. xxx.
\item[\gunavati] \textit{Guṇavatī} of Ratnākaraśānti (fl. early 11th century), a commentary on the \textit{Mahāmāyātantra}. Ed. Samdhong Rinpoche \& Vrajavallabh Dwivedi, \textit{Mahāmāyātantram with Guṇavatī by Ratnākaraśānti}. Rare Buddhist Texts Series 10. Central Insitute of Higher Tibetan Studies, Sarnath 1992.
\item[\dipamkarabhadra] \textit{Guhyasamājamaṇḍalavidhi} (also known as the \textit{Sārdhatriśatikā}) of Dīpaṃkarabhadra (fl. first half of the 9th century). Ed. \textit{Dhīḥ. Journal of Rare Buddhist Texts Research Unit} 42, 2006. pp. 109-154. • Ms. G Niedersächsische Staats- und Universitätsbibliothek Göttingen, Cod. ms. sanscr. 257. Palm-leaf, ff. xx (last folio missing), Māgadhī, undated (mid 11th century). • Ms. C Cambridge University Library Or. 132. Palm-leaf, ff. 24 (first folio missing), Old Newar, undated.
\item[\caturyoginisamputa] \textit{Caturyoginīsaṃpuṭa}, an explanatory tantra of the Cakrasaṃvara cycle. Ms. listed in \textsc{Sferra} 2008: 51, item 13. Palm-leaf, ff. 7 (only rectos are legible), Kuṭilā, undated. • \Td\ Tōh. no. 376. Tr. by Chings Yon tan 'bar.
\item[\cakrasamvaravivrti] \textit{Cakrasaṃvaravivṛti} of Bhavabhaṭṭa (fl. first half of the 10th century), a commentary on the \textit{Herukābhidhāna}. Ed. S Janardan Shastri Pandey, \textit{Śrīherukābhidhānam Cakrasaṃvaratantram with the Vivṛti Commentary of Bhavabhaṭṭa}. Vols. I-II. Rare Buddhist Texts Series 26. Central Insitute of Higher Tibetan Studies, Sarnath 2002.
\item[\cittavisuddhiprakarana] \textit{Cittaviśuddhiprakaraṇa} of Āryadeva (fl. 9th century). Ed. Prabhubhai Bhikhabhai Patel, \textit{Cittavisuddhiprakarana of Aryadeva. Sanskrit and Tibetan Texts.} Visva-bharati, 1949. • Ms. NAK 1-1697 = NGMPP B 24/21. Palm-leaf, ff. 9 (one folio missing), Old Newar, undated.
\item[\jnanasiddhi] \textit{Jñānasiddhi} of Indrabhūti (fl. late 8th and early 9th century). Ed. B Benoytosh Bhattacharyya, \textit{Two Vajrayāna Works. Edited with an Introduction and Index.} Oriental Institute, Baroda 1929. • Ed. S Samdhong Rinpoche \& Vrajvallabh Dwivedi, \textit{Guhyādi-Aṣṭasiddhi-saṅgraha}. Rare Buddhist Text [sic!] Series 1. Central Insitute of Higher Tibetan Studies, Sarnath 1987. • Ms. NAK 5-45 = NGMPP A 915/3.
\item[\dakarnava] \textit{Ḍākārṇavatantra}, an explanatory tantra of the Cakrasaṃvara cycle.
\item[\tattvasiddhi] \textit{Tattvasiddhi} of pseudo-Śāntarakṣita.
\item[\trisamaya] \textit{Trisamayarājatantra} (also known as \textit{*Trisamayavyūha}, \textit{Trisamayarājakalpa}). Tōh. 502. Tr. by Kṛṣṇapaṇḍita and Tshul khrims rgyal ba.
\item[\ratnakaragupta] \textit{Trisamayarājasādhana} by Ratnākaragupta. Ed. \textit{Sādhanamālā} no. 2. • Ms. NAK 3-387 = NGMPP B 24/11 (8\recto6'-9\verso3').
\item[\vilasavajra] \textit{Nāmamantrārthāvalokinī} of Vilāsavajra (fl. late 8th, perhaps also early 9th century), a commentary on the \textit{Mañjuśrīnāmasaṃgīti}. Ed. Anthony Tribe, \textit{The Names of Wisdom. A Critical Edition and Annotated Translation of Chapter 1-5 of Vilāsavajra's Commentary on the Nāmasaṃgīti, with Introduction and Textual Notes}, doctoral dissertation, University of Oxford 1994. • Ms. A Cambridge University Library Add. 1708. Palm-leaf, ff. 115 (26 missing), Old Newar, dated 1391 or 1457 CE. • Ms. B Private collection of P. R. Vajrācārya = NGMPP E 360/16. Paper, ff. 86, Common Newar, undated.
\item[\pancakrama] \textit{Pañcakrama}. Ed. Katsumi Mimaki \& Tōru Tomabechi, \textit{Pañcakrama. Sanskrit and Tibetan Texts Critically Edited with Verse Index and Facsimile Edition of the Sanskrit Manuscripts.} Bibliotheca Codicum Asiaticorum 8. The Centre for East Asian Cultural Studies for Unesco, Tokyo 1994.
\item[\pradipoddyotana] \textit{Pradīpoddyotana} of Candrakīrti (fl. 9th century), a commentary on the \textit{Guhyasamājatantra}. Ed. Chintaharan Chakravarti, \textit{Guhyasamājatantrapradīpodyotanaṭīkāṣaṭkoṭivyākhyā}. Tibetan Sanskrit Works Series No. 25. Kashi Prasad Jayaswal Research Institute, Patna 1984.
\item[\bhramahara] \textit{Bhramaharanāma Hevajrasādhana} of Ratnākaraśānti (fl. early 11th century). Ed. Harunaga Isaacson, ``Ratnākaraśānti's \textit{Bhramaharanāma Hevajrasādhana: Critical Edition (Studies in Ratnākaraśānti's tantric works III),'' in \textit{Kokusai Bukkyogaku Daigakuin Daigaku kenkyu kiyo} 5, 2002, 151(80)-176(55).}
\item[\vajrajvalodaya] \textit{Vajrajvālodayā sādhanopayikā} of Ānandagarbha (fl. 9th century). Ms. Göttingen Xc 14/39, ff. 170\recto6--186\recto3, for a description of which see Isaacson 2009. Partially edited in Sanderson 2009.
\item[\vajradaka] \textit{Vajraḍākatantra}, an explanatory tantra of the Cakrasaṃvara cycle. Ed. Tsunehiko Sugiki, "A Critical Study of The Vajraḍākamahātantrarāja (I) Chapter. 1 and 42." Journal of Chisan Studies 65, 2002. pp. 81-115. • Ms T • Ms C. % also include his other article, since we note parallel to ch. 36!
\item[\vajrasattvasadhana] \textit{Vajrasattvaniṣpādanasūtra} of Candrakīrti (fl. 9th century). Ed. Luo Hong and Toru Tomabechi, \textit{Candrakīrti's Vajrasattvaniṣpādanasūtra (Vajrasattvasādhana). Sanskrit and Tibetan Texts.} Sanskrit Texts from the Tibetan Autonomous Region No. 6. China Tibetology Publishing House \& Austrian Academy of Sciences Press, Beijing-Vienna 2009.
\item[\vajramanda] \textit{Vajramaṇḍālaṃkāra}. Tōh. 490. Tr. began by Dpal ldan chos rje, continued by Blo gros brtan pa.
\item[\vyavastholi] \textit{Vyavastholi} of Nāgabuddhi (fl. 9th century). Ed. Kimiaki Tanaka, "Nāgabodhi’s *Samājasādhanavyavasthālī: The Tibetan Translation and Sanskrit Text of Chapter II" [in Japanese]. \textit{Tōyō Bunka Kenkyūjo Kiyō} 160, 2011, pp. 324(313)–338(299).
\item[\samputa] \textit{Saṃpuṭodbhavatantra}, an explanatory tantra of the Cakrasaṃvara and other cycles. Edited from Mss.
\item[\samputatilaka] \textit{Saṃpuṭatilaka}, an appendix to the \textit{Saṃpuṭodbhavatantra}. Edited from Mss. %\ras\wel now add C2!
\item[\bhuvacarya] \textit{Saṃvarodayā nāma maṇḍalopāyikā} of Bhūvācārya (fl. before 1054 at Ratnagiri). Ms. Tōkyō University Library New no. 450 = Old no. 296. Palm-leaf, ff. 56 (20, 53 lost or missing), Old Newar, dated 1054 CE.
\item[\nagabuddhi] \textit{Samājamaṇḍalopāyikā} of Nāgabuddhi (fl. 9th century). Ed. Kimiaki Tanaka, \textit{Indo ni okeru Mandara no Seiritsu to Hatten}, [...] Tokyo 2010. pp. 631-716. • Ms. listed in \textsc{Sferra} 2008: 45, item 33. Palm-leaf, ff. 7 (6\recto not photographed), Māgadhī, undated (most likely 12th century, copied at Vikramaśīla).
\item[\saramanjarip] \textit{Sāramañjarī}, a commentary to Jñānapāda's \textit{Samanta\-bhadra} (a.k.a. \textit{Caturaṅgasādhana}) by Samantabhadra as transmitted in the manuscript photographed by Giuseppe Tucci. [...]
\item[\cmp] \textit{Sūtaka} or \textit{Sūtakamelāpaka} (better known as \textit{*Caryāmelāpakapradīpa}, but this title is not attested in Sanskrit) of Āryadeva (fl. 9th century). Ed. S Janardan Shastri Pandey, \textit{Caryāmelāpakapradīpam of Ācārya Āryadeva}. Rare Buddhist Texts Series 22. Central Institute of Higher Tibetan Studies, Sarnath 2000. • Ed. W Christian Wedemeyer, \textit{Āryadeva's Lamp that Integrates the Practices (Caryāmelāpakapradīpa). The Gradual Path of Vajrayāna Buddhism According to the Esoteric Community Noble Tradition.} Treasury of the Buddhist Sciences series. Columbia University Press, New York 2007.
\item[\svadhisthanakramaprabheda] \textit{Svādhiṣṭhānakramaprabheda} of Āryadeva (fl. 9th century). Ed. Janardan Shastri Pandey, \textit{Bauddhalaghugranthasaṃgraha / A Collection of Minor Buddhist Texts}, Central Insitute of Higher Tibetan Studies, Sarnath 1997. pp. 169-177. • Ms. Tōkyō University Library New no. 517 = Old no. 557. Palm-leaf, ff. 3 (the present fragment, only vv. 15c-58), Māgadhī, undated.
\item[\herukabhidhana] \textit{Herukābhidhāna} (also known as the \textit{Laghuśaṃvara}, the \textit{Cakrasaṃvara}, or just \textit{Saṃvara}). Ed. David B. Gray, \textit{The Cakrasamvara Tantra (The Discourse of Śrī Heruka). Editions of the Sanskrit and Tibetan Texts.} Treasury of Buddhist Sciences. The American Institute of Buddhist Studies \& Columbia University Center for Buddhist Studies \& Tibet House US, New York, 2012.
\item[\hevajratantra] \textit{Hevajratantra}. Ed.
\end{description}
\newpage
%Tōh. 366.
%Rgyud Ka 151b1-193a
%Sarvabuddhasamāyogaḍākinījālasaṃvara
% Dalton-van Schaik 2006, 454/1 (in quotations) & 716/2 (deities)
% van Schaik, Early Days has some discussion of this
% as does Weinberger 2003, seems rather confused
% Davidson 1981 (could not locate ref. from van Schaik)
% Sanderson 2009: 145-156
% for an early attestation see — *Prajñāpāramitānayādhyardhaśatikāṭīkā ('Phags pa shes rab kyi pha rol tu phyin pa tshul brgya lnga bcu pa'i 'grel pa). Tôh. no. 2647. Dergé Tanjur, vol. JU, folios 272v.7 -294r.5. —	Davidson, IEB, pp. 242-243. —	Yukio Hatta, Index to the Ārya- Prajñāpāramitā- Naya- Śatapañcaśaṭīkā, Heirakuji-shoten (Kyoto 1971). —	Shuyu Kanaoka, Kukurāja, Indogaku Bukkyôgaku Kenkyû, vol. 15, no. 1 (December 1966), pp. 8-17.
% A translation of this commentary by Jñānamitra is already mentioned in the Ldan/Lhan kar ma catalogue (cf. Lalou 1953: 331, no. 523: tshul brgya lnga bcu'i 'grel pa | slob dpon Jñānamitras mdzad pa | 600 śl. | 2 bp ||). This catalogue is commonly assigned to the reign of Khri lde srong btsan/Sad na legs, i.e. ca. 800-815 CE (Halkias 2004: 48), but the dating is not uncontroversial (see ibid. n. 4). Jñānamitra's work contains a narrative summarized by Weinberger (2003: 235-237) involving [an] Indrabhūti and the siddha Kukkurāja. The Sarvabuddhasamāyoga is discussed here. [NB: the translation, even as preserved in more modern gsar ma canons, definitely has an archaic feel to it. This is not only evident from the way Sanskrit words are transcribed (very reminiscent of the haphazard Dunhuang fashion: de byi for devī), e.g. guhya samanytsa for Guhyasamāja, etc., but also some archaisms such as the -o cog plural.] Much of this is also discussed in IEB 242-245. Elements of the story (namely, Indrabhūti's accomplishment with his harem) are alluded to in the CMP (ed. Wedemeyer, p. 477: ... yoginībhiḥ saha ramamāṇo yathā rājā Indrabhūtiḥ tadvat kalevaraṃ parivartya vajrakāyo bhūtvāntaḥpureṇa sahāntardhāyāṣṭaguṇaiśvaryānvito buddhakṣetrād buddhakṣetraṃ gacchati |.
% Amoghavajra gives the title of the text in his list (Taishō 869), which dates from 746-774 CE. Sanderson 2009: 145, n. 336 (ref. also to Tomabechi 2007) dates his knowledge of the text to 741-746, when he was in Ceylon and perhaps India.
% Scriptural material related to the Sarvabuddhasamāyogaḍākinījālaśaṃvara amounts to two titles, which are to be found in the sDe dge canon in volume Ka of the Rgyud section (Tōh. nos. 366 and 367) [= Peking Rgyud Ka, Ōtani nos. 8 and 9]. The first is the tantra in question in ten chapters (here referred to as an *uttaratantra - probably by error), whereas the second is the *Sarvakalpasamuccaya (here referred to as an *uttarottaratantra) in five chapters (numbered 18 to 22). The latter too must be an original Indic work, since we have a commentary on it by Ānandagarbha, and some testimonia (quoted [not yet traced] and referred to once each in the Caryāmelāpakapradīpa and the Tattvasiddhi). According to the testimony of *Pramuditavajra (see below) and Bu ston Rin chen grub, there existed yet another tantra of this cycle in seven chapters. Bu ston even states that there were in his time (early 14th century) rumours of an early translation of this text, but that it cannot be found anymore (\textit{\ldots\ sngar 'gyur par grags te da lta ma rnyed do ||}). This, apparently, would account for the strange numeration of the *Sarvakalpasamuccaya, since seven chapters make up the gap between the ten chapters of the root-tantra, and the first—numbered eighteenth—of the continuation-tantra.
% Exegetical material related to the Sarvabuddhasamāyogaḍākinījālaśaṃvara are organized in the sDe dge canon in volumes Ra and La of the Rgyud 'grel section (Tōh. nos. 1659-1682) [Peking Rgyud 'grel volumes Ya and Ra].
% Tōh. 1659 [= Ōta. 2531] is the longest commentary, of one Brgya byin sdang/sdong po (*Indranāla)—according to the testimony of the colophon, from Oḍiyāna and a king—, but this is most likely not the translation of an Indic work, but rather a Tibetan composition. The author cites several tantras that are otherwise unknown in Indic sources, but which are very popular in the rNying ma tradition (e.g. the Thabs kyi zhags pa).
% Tōh. 1660 [= Ōta. 2532] is a *vṛtti by *Pramuditavajra. For the time being not much is known of this author, but he is presumably not very early, since he already knows of and discusses the notorious Fourth Initiation (caturthābhiṣeka). He also refers to previous commentators, namely Ānandagarbha and *Praśāntamitra.
% Tōh. 1661 [= Ōta. 2533] is a *pañjikā attributed to (one of the) Indrabhūti(s) in the colophon, but it very much seems from the introductory verses that the composition is that of *Śākyamitra, following his queries to the aforementioned Indrabhūti.
% Tōh. 1662 [= Ōta. 2534] is a commentary to the *uttaratantra, the *Sarvakalpasamuccaya, by the well-known yogatantra exegete, Ānandagarbha (floruit 9th c.). If the colophon statement is original, then the work was composed at Nālandā. There is some circumstantial evidence to the effect that Ānandagarbha produced a commentary to the present text as well, but this is not available to us in any form.
% Tōh. 1663 [= Ōta. 2535] is another *pañjikā, by one *Praśāntamitra. He is probably not to be confused with the translator under the same name. Tradition stemming from India identify him as a student of Jñānapāda, and a contemporary of Dīpaṃkarabhadra. His floruit thus most likely falls somewhere in the middle of the ninth century. He is also a commentator of the Māyājāla (Tōh. 2514) and the Vajramaṇḍālaṃkāra (Tōh. 2515) (the dedication verses are very similar to the one in the commentary relevant here; all three most likely contained the phrase `saha praśāntamitreṇa'). For the time being I cannot find any quoted or referred text in his work that would place him any time later than the range proposed above.
% The three latter commentaries (Tōh. nos. 1661-63) were all translated by persons who can be relatively safely dated to the earlier half of the eleventh century.
% Tōh. 1664-1671 [= Ōta. 2536-2543] are all attributed to the siddha Kukurāja; these are sādhanas and an initiation manual. It is noteworthy that the latter also speaks of the Fourth Initiation. Tōh. 1672 [= Ōta. 2544] is a *gaṇa[cakra]vidhi attributed to (an) Indrabhūti. This work has been partially translated and studied to some extent by Davidson (2002: ~318-319). The remaining texts are all sādhanas, most of which are attributed to one *Hūṃkāravajra (and variants of this name). An exception is another initiation manual, Tōh. no. 1679 [= Ōta. 2551], which is attributed to one *Ratnavajra, possibly the same as the eleventh-century Kashmiri author. I would not favour this identification, since this *Ratnavajra discusses and endorses the Fourth Initiation, whereas we know from the Kashmiri *Ratnavajra's other works and testimonia that he was not ready to accept its existence and vigorously debated `Eastern paṇḍitas' (by which he meant, first and foremost, Vāgīśvarakīrti) who were, at least to him, its chief proponents. It is noteable that this *Ratnavajra states in the second introductory verse that (by his time) the tantra and its system has become almost extinct (\textit{kun du nub po nyid mthong nas}), which is one of the reasons for his writing the manual.
% Peking: vol. Ka, djvu page 182
% col. in rNying ma rgyud 'bum:
%rgya gar gyi mkhas po badzra ha sa dang| sgyu 'phrul gyi lo tsa ba Sma Rin chen mchog gis 'grel pa bzhi'i gzhung dang bstun te| zhu chen dang bcas par gtan la phab pa'o|| sangs rgyas mnyam sbyor la rtogs pa nyi shu rtsa gnyis pa'i rgyud yod ces pa phyogs gcig tu bsdus pa rtog| sa bcu pa'i rgyud 'di yin| rgyud phyi ma rtog pa bdun pa yod| phyi ma'i phyi ma rtog pa bsdus pa'i rgyud yod pa dang| cha mthun gyi rgyud rdo rje gdan bzhi'i rgyud rgyud yin no||
% acc. to Tōhoku this is an uttaratantra, tr. by Lha rin po che (although I can't see this in Derge col.)

%@ namaḥ śrīvajrasattvāya ||
%rgya gar skad du | shrī sarba buddha sa ma yo ga ḍā ki nī dzā la samba ra nā ma utta ra tantra | bod skad du | dpal sangs rgyas thams cad dang mnyam par sbyor ba mkha' 'gro ma sgyu ma bde ba'i mchog ces bya ba'i rgyud phyi ma || dpal rdo rje sems dpa' la phyag 'tshal lo ||

rahasye parame ramye sarvātmani sadā sthitaḥ \dandab % ø

% prae. obscure?
% always located in everything

\startcomm

bauddhāḥ pāramitāḥ siddhā dhāraṇyo bhūmayas tathā \dandab % |
\vvar{\va siddhā] \corr, siddhāḥ \cod} % or sandhi in pausa between pādas
% ab] incorporated in Dīpaṃkarabhadra's Maṇḍalavidhi (v `+11' in CUL Or. 132)
% of a Buddha
% cf. Svādhiṣṭhānakramaprabheda 53: dehabhogapratiṣṭhānabhūmipāramitādayaḥ | buddhaṛddhivikurvādi sarvaṃ māyāviceṣṭitam ||
ramante kiṃ punar nāryaḥ subhagā divyamānuṣāḥ \veg % ||
\vvar{\vd -mānuṣāḥ] \eme, -māntaṣāḥ \cod}
%sangs rgyas pha rol phyin grub pa |
%gzungs dang sa rnams de bzhin du |
%dga' bar spyod na lha dang (D 154b5) mi'i |
%skal bzang bud med ci zhig smos || 4.4 ||

\vers % *Praśāntamitra: dpal he ru ka'i 'byung ba bstan pa'i phyir --->

yaḥ sādhayen māṃ svasukhair na duḥkhaiḥ \veg
